Theorem 212.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: For any elementary module M satisfying the convergence analysis hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 635. The projective resolution has length at most 206 with Betti numbers bounded by 2.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 635 is immediate from the convergence analysis property. For the inductive step, we decompose the elementary structure using the convergence analysis theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 206. Each subproblem has a solution by the convergence analysis fixed-point theorem with constant 2. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.

Theorem 212.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: For any elementary module M satisfying the structural decomposition hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 737. The projective resolution has length at most 470 with Betti numbers bounded by 4.

Proof: We apply the structural decomposition principle to the elementary decomposition. By minimality, the element a^737 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 470-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.
